### TL;DR

The paper demonstrates high-rate entanglement swapping between independent entanglement sources over 17.6 km of deployed commercial fiber in New York City, establishing a scalable architecture for metropolitan quantum networks.

### Method

### Result

### Conclusion

This work provides a practical demonstration of entanglement swapping over commercial fiber, supporting the development of scalable metropolitan quantum networks and future quantum repeater architectures.

## 2. Marginal-constrained Entropy Accumulation Theorem

- **Score**: 8.8 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-11-18
- **Authors**: Amir Arqand And Ernest Y.-Z. Tan
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-18-2273/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-18-2273/)
- **Tags**: `entropy accumulation theorem`  `Rényi entropy`  `channel conditional entropy`  `QKD security proof`  `adaptive protocols`

### TL;DR

The paper proves a marginal-constrained entropy accumulation theorem via a new chain rule for channel conditional entropies, enabling fully adaptive security proofs for prepare-and-measure QKD.

### Motivation

Entropy accumulation is central to composable security proofs in quantum cryptography, but existing variants have limitations on input-state constraints and protocol adaptivity.

### Method

The authors derive a chain rule for a family of channel conditional entropies covering von Neumann and sandwiched Rényi entropies, show equality with their regularized versions, and apply this to sequences of channels to obtain a new Rényi entropy accumulation theorem with marginal-state constraints.

### Result

The resulting marginal-constrained EAT generalizes a recent security-proof technique for prepare-and-measure QKD without repetition-rate limitations and supports fully adaptive protocols that can update entropy estimation during the protocol.

### Conclusion

This work strengthens the entropy accumulation toolkit for quantum cryptography by relaxing constraints and enabling adaptive security analyses, with direct implications for QKD security proofs.

*Keywords: entropy accumulation theorem, Rényi entropy, channel conditional entropy, QKD security proof, adaptive protocols*

### TL;DR

The paper proves that tripartite Haar-random quantum states contain no bipartite Einstein-Podolsky-Rosen entanglement that can be distilled by local operations.

### Motivation

Understanding entanglement structure in random multipartite states is important for quantum information theory, especially for characterizing distillable bipartite entanglement in complex quantum states.

### Method

The authors analyze tripartite Haar-random states and use local operations to test whether bipartite EPR entanglement can be extracted from them.

### Result

They demonstrate that no bipartite EPR entanglement can be distilled from tripartite Haar-random states under local operations.

### Conclusion

This establishes a strong limitation on bipartite entanglement distillation from generic tripartite random states, clarifying the multipartite entanglement structure of Haar-random states.

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `non-Gaussian entanglement`  `stellar rank`  `bosonic states`  `separability criteria`

### TL;DR

The paper develops a geometric framework for characterizing non-Gaussian entanglement in bosonic states with finite stellar rank using stellar polynomials, essential variables, and structural graphs.

### Motivation

Non-Gaussian entanglement in continuous-variable bosonic systems is a key resource but lacks a general structural characterization comparable to Gaussian entanglement theory.

### Method

The authors introduce atomic decompositions of stellar polynomials and associated structural graphs, define essential variables corresponding to symplectic rank, and derive separability criteria via hyperplane decompositions of zero sets.

### Result

They obtain complete separability criteria for two-mode states and for stellar-rank-2 states with arbitrary mode number, and show how connected components of the structural graph determine mode-intrinsic entanglement and passive-separability-compatible partitions.

### Conclusion

The framework isolates genuinely non-Gaussian entanglement resources and quantifies preparation complexity, providing a structural foundation for entanglement theory beyond Gaussian states.

*Keywords: non-Gaussian entanglement, stellar rank, bosonic states, separability criteria, entanglement structure*

- **Tags**: `post-selection games`  `nonlocal games`  `Bell nonlocality`  `Tsirelson bound`  `Hardy's paradox`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces post-selection games, a generalization of nonlocal games with discarding outcomes, and develops methods to compute their local and Tsirelson bounds for Bell nonlocality analysis.

### Motivation

Standard nonlocal games do not naturally capture possibilistic nonlocality proofs such as Hardy's paradox or Bell tests with low detection efficiency, where some rounds are discarded.

### Method

The authors define post-selection games with win/lose/discard outcomes, develop algorithms for computing local and Tsirelson bounds, and analyze their statistical power compared with traditional nonlocal games.

### Result

Post-selection games can provide an unbounded statistical-power advantage over traditional nonlocal games and are well suited for analyzing Bell tests with low detection efficiency.

### Conclusion

Post-selection games offer a useful framework for possibilistic nonlocality proofs and detection-efficiency-limited Bell experiments, extending the theory of nonlocal games in a practically relevant direction.

*Keywords: post-selection games, nonlocal games, Bell nonlocality, Tsirelson bound, Hardy's paradox*

### Method

### Result

### Conclusion

### Motivation

### Result

For a broad class of LDOI bases, including all two-qubit cases, the LOCC supremum equals the PPT and separable optima, and more generally the PPT-LOCC distinguishability gap is bounded by a small dimension-dependent quantity.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

Analytical study of Motzkin spin chains uncovers unusual symmetries, correlation scaling, and entanglement entropy scaling that suggest a new universality class and challenge standard tensor-network descriptions.

### Motivation

Motzkin spin chains are gapless 1D models without a CFT description, and colorful variants exhibit power-law violation of the entanglement area law, making their low-energy and entanglement structure interesting but not directly aligned with core quantum information protocols.

### Method

The authors analytically derive symmetry structures, spin-spin correlation functions, and asymptotic entanglement scaling expressions with explicit additive constants for colorless and colorful Motzkin chains.

### Result

The integer-spin chain shows spontaneous symmetry breaking and ferromagnetic order, while colorful chains display quasi-long-range algebraic order with non-BKT exponents; entanglement scaling implies superpolynomial matrix-product-state bond dimension.

### Conclusion

Motzkin spin chains provide analytically tractable testbeds for quantum simulation of many-body physics and highlight limitations of standard 1D tensor-network methods.

*Keywords: Motzkin spin chains, entanglement entropy, area-law violation, matrix product states, spin chains*

## 143. Quantum Channel Testing In Average-Case Distance

- **Score**: 4.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-11-18
- **Authors**: Gregory Rosenthal, Hugo Aaronson, Sathyawageeswar Subramanian, Animesh Datta, And Tom Gur
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-18-2276/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-18-2276/)
- **Tags**: `adaptivity`  `quantum channel testing`  `diamond norm`  `property testing`  `query complexity`

### TL;DR

The paper studies the query complexity of testing properties of quantum channels, showing diamond-norm identity testing is inherently hard and introducing an average-case analogue (the ACID norm) that permits dimension-independent testing in restricted models.

### Motivation

Diamond-norm distance is worst-case and thus forces query complexity to scale with channel dimension, motivating both a lower-bound analysis and a more practical average-case distance measure for channel certification.

### Method

Proves query lower bounds for identity testing in diamond norm across the strongest (ancilla-assisted, adaptive, coherent) algorithmic models, then defines the average-case imitation diamond (ACID) norm and analyzes testing and tomography complexity under it in weaker models without ancillae or adaptivity.

### Result

Identity testing in diamond norm requires dimension-dependent queries regardless of model, whereas in ACID distance some channel classes can be tested with dimension-independent complexity (others depend on input/output dimensions); tight bounds on ACID-distance channel tomography are also established.

### Conclusion

Average-case channel distance measures offer a viable route to efficient channel certification where worst-case diamond-norm testing is provably intractable, sharpening the complexity landscape of quantum channel testing.

*Keywords: quantum channel testing, diamond norm, property testing, query complexity, channel tomography*

---

- **Tags**: `Trotter error`  `product formulas`  `multiproduct formulas`  `Floquet analysis`  `Hamiltonian simulation`

### TL;DR

A Floquet-based framework yields commutator-scaling error bounds for time-dependent product and multiproduct Trotter formulas, tightening gate-count estimates for simulating driven dynamics and adiabatic state preparation.

### Motivation

Product formulas are the workhorse of digital quantum simulation, but rigorous error bounds for time-dependent Hamiltonians — especially multiproduct extrapolation schemes — were less developed than in the time-independent case, leaving gate-count estimates loose.

### Method

The authors recast the stroboscopic dynamics of product formulas as a Floquet problem, deriving error bounds that scale with nested commutators of the Hamiltonian and its time derivatives, and extend the analysis to multiproduct (Richardson-type) extrapolations.

### Result

The derived bounds are sharper than prior Magnus-based estimates and translate into improved gate-count scalings for driven-dynamics simulation and adiabatic state preparation.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical analytical toolkit for bounding Trotter and multiproduct errors in time-dependent settings, refining resource estimates for digital quantum simulation algorithms.

*Keywords: Trotter error, product formulas, multiproduct formulas, Floquet analysis, Hamiltonian simulation*

- **Tags**: `quantum data re-uploading`  `quantum machine learning`  `universal function approximation`  `quantum inputs`  `collision models`

### TL;DR

The paper proposes a quantum data re-uploading architecture that processes quantum inputs by sequentially interacting a qubit with fresh copies of an input state, enabling universal approximation of bounded continuous functions.

### Motivation

Data re-uploading is well established for classical inputs, but its extension to quantum inputs is underexplored because quantum information cannot be directly accessed classically.

### Method

A small quantum circuit repeatedly couples an ancilla qubit to copies of an arbitrary input state, alternating entangling unitaries with mid-circuit resets of the input register to realize a discrete cascade of completely positive trace-preserving maps.

### Result

The architecture can approximate any bounded continuous function using one ancilla qubit and single-qubit measurements, given sufficiently many input copies and layers.

### Conclusion

The work offers a space-efficient and expressive framework for quantum machine learning directly on quantum data, analogous to collision models in open quantum systems.

*Keywords: quantum data re-uploading, quantum machine learning, universal function approximation, quantum inputs, collision models*

### Motivation

Time-dependent MPS algorithms such as time-evolving block decimation typically suffer from accumulating Trotter error and slow convergence for moderate time steps, limiting their use for driven many-body spin systems.

### Result

- **Tags**: `epr`  `unitary cluster Jastrow ansatz`  `variational quantum eigensolver`  `quantum chemistry`  `compressed double factorization`

### TL;DR

The authors propose two classical-preprocessing methods to improve parameter initialization for the unitary cluster Jastrow (UCJ) and local UCJ ansatzes used in variational quantum algorithms for quantum chemistry.

### Motivation

Truncating ansatz repetitions and discarding interactions to satisfy hardware connectivity degrades the CCSD-derived initialization of UCJ/LUCJ circuits, reducing achievable energy accuracy on near-term devices.

### Method

One method uses compressed double factorization of CCSD amplitudes to recover the CCSD approximation for both expectation-value- and sample-based algorithms; the other uses approximate tensor-network simulation to improve sample quality for sample-based algorithms.

### Result

Validation via exact state-vector simulation up to 52 qubits and experiments on superconducting processors up to 65 qubits shows the initialization methods significantly improve the output of both expectation-value- and sample-based algorithms.

### Conclusion

Classical preprocessing of UCJ/LUCJ initial parameters can substantially mitigate hardware-connectivity-induced accuracy loss, though quantum-measurement-based parameter optimization is left to future work.

*Keywords: unitary cluster Jastrow ansatz, variational quantum eigensolver, quantum chemistry, compressed double factorization, parameter initialization*

## 203. Below-shot-noise Capacity In Phase Estimation Using Nonlinear Interferometers

- **Score**: 1.7 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-11-18
- **Authors**: Cristofero Oglialoro, Gerard J. Machado, Felix Farsch, Daniel F. Urrego, Alejandra A. Padilla, Raj B. Patel, Ian A. Walmsley, Markus Gräfe, Juan P. Torres, And Enno Giese
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-18-2272/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-18-2272/)
- **Tags**: `heisenberg-limit`  `aging`  `shot-noise`  `quantum-imaging`  `nonlinear interferometry`

### TL;DR

The paper compares three nonlinear interferometer configurations for phase estimation using intensity measurements, finding that Mandel-type differential detection is the most robust but does not surpass the shot-noise limit, while Yurke-type setups can achieve sub-shot-noise sensitivity only under restrictive loss and gain conditions.

### Motivation

Nonlinear interferometers are attractive for bicolor quantum imaging and sensing with undetected light and can in principle provide quantum-enhanced sensitivity, but their practical advantage under realistic detection and loss is unclear.

### Method

The authors theoretically analyze and compare a Yurke-type SU(1,1) interferometer, a Mandel-type induced-coherence interferometer, and a hybrid scheme using easily accessible intensity-based measurements for phase estimation, including the effects of loss.

### Result

Differential intensity detection in the Mandel interferometer gives the highest and most robust phase sensitivity, reaching but not surpassing the shot-noise limit even with loss; Yurke-type intensity measurements can show genuine sub-shot-noise sensitivity under balanced losses and moderate gain but degrade at realistic high gain.

### Conclusion

For realistic high-gain regimes with loss, the Mandel configuration with differential detection is the most robust approach, while the Heisenberg-like advantage of the ideal Yurke interferometer is fragile under practical constraints.

*Keywords: nonlinear interferometry, phase estimation, sub-shot-noise sensitivity, SU(1,1) interferometer, quantum imaging*

---

Classical dynamical-systems analysis of billiard trajectories with parabolic focusing and dispersing boundaries, using phase-space portraits, Poincaré sections, and characterization of regular islands, chaotic seas, and the transition to full chaos as a function of the boundary deformation parameter.

- **Tags**: `relaxation`  `electron hydrodynamics`  `tomographic transport`  `dual-relaxation-time approximation`  `boundary layers`

### TL;DR

A new asymptotic transport theory shows that electron flow in clean confined geometries is neither purely ballistic nor hydrodynamic, but a distinct regime where long-lived odd (tomographic) modes modify bulk flow and generate strong boundary layers.

### Motivation

The dual-relaxation-time approximation, which treats momentum-conserving and momentum-relaxing scattering with two rates, is insufficient to capture electron transport in high-quality materials where viscous and ballistic effects coexist.

### Method

Analytical asymptotic theory of the linearized Boltzmann/continuum hydrodynamic equations for electron fluids, extended beyond the dual-relaxation-time ansatz to include long-lived odd-parity modes, with boundary-layer analysis near device edges.

### Result

Odd modes are found to substantially alter bulk hydrodynamic flow and induce pronounced boundary layers at device boundaries, defining a transport regime distinct from both ballistic and hydrodynamic limits.

### Conclusion

Tomographic electron flow in confined geometries requires going beyond the dual-relaxation-time approximation; the predicted boundary-layer signatures offer guidance for interpreting spatially resolved transport experiments in clean electron fluids.

*Keywords: electron hydrodynamics, tomographic transport, dual-relaxation-time approximation, boundary layers, Boltzmann transport*

### TL;DR

The paper derives a fully nonequilibrium intrinsic Hall response driven by Berry curvature in time-reversal-symmetric, inversion-asymmetric topological materials.

### Motivation

Spontaneous zero-field Hall effects in topological materials are usually treated in near-equilibrium or linear-response regimes, which is inadequate for strongly correlated Weyl-Kondo semimetals where inelastic scattering and strong interactions dominate.

### Method

The authors formulate a nonequilibrium Hall conductivity from Berry curvature using a quantum Boltzmann/Green's-function treatment that retains inelastic scattering and interaction effects beyond the usual relaxation-time approximation.

### Result

They obtain a Berry-curvature Hall response that survives arbitrarily strong nonequilibrium conditions, clarifying how intrinsic Hall signals emerge and how they differ from conventional Drude-like transport.

### Conclusion

The framework provides a theoretical basis for interpreting spontaneous Hall experiments in correlated topological semimetals such as Weyl-Kondo systems.

### Motivation

Moiré superlattices host tunable excitonic states, but achieving and understanding the strong-moiré regime where different exciton species mix requires an external control knob that continuously modifies the moiré potential.

### Method

Hydrostatic pressure is applied to semiconductor moiré superlattices to continuously tune the moiré potential, with optical spectroscopy probing the resulting excitonic resonances.

### Result

The data show a pressure-driven crossover into a strong-moiré regime in which Wannier-Rydberg excitons and charge-transfer excitons hybridize into new mixed moiré exciton states.

### Conclusion

Pressure provides an effective, continuous tuning parameter for moiré exciton physics, enabling access to hybridization regimes relevant for engineered excitonic and optoelectronic phenomena in superlattices.

*Keywords: moiré excitons, hydrostatic pressure, Wannier-Rydberg excitons, charge-transfer excitons, semiconductor superlattices*

- **Tags**: `domain walls`  `primordial magnetic fields`  `Chern-Simons coupling`  `Peccei-Quinn symmetry`  `axion cosmology`

### TL;DR

A cosmological mechanism in which a helical primordial magnetic field supplies a spatially inhomogeneous Chern-Simons source that biases and destabilizes stable domain walls, solving the domain wall problem without an explicit Peccei-Quinn bias term.

### Motivation

Stable domain walls would overclose the Universe, so they must decay; the standard remedy is to break the Peccei-Quinn symmetry with an ad hoc bias term, which the authors aim to replace with a dynamical, field-based mechanism.

### Method

The authors construct a model in which a helical primordial magnetic field couples to the axion sector through a Chern-Simons term, producing an inhomogeneous potential bias on the domain-wall network, and they analyze the resulting wall dynamics and annihilation.

### Result

The induced Chern-Simons bias is shown to be sufficient to drive domain walls to decay, alleviating the overclosure problem while simultaneously linking the wall dynamics to the presence of helical magnetic fields.

### Conclusion

Primordial magnetic fields can act as a dynamical solution to the domain wall problem, offering a cosmologically motivated alternative to explicit Peccei-Quinn symmetry breaking and potentially leaving observable imprints tied to magnetogenesis.

*Keywords: domain walls, primordial magnetic fields, Chern-Simons coupling, Peccei-Quinn symmetry, axion cosmology*

Diffractive events are identified by forward scattering and large rapidity gaps, but ordinary hard QCD scattering also produces gapped events, creating a serious background problem for diffractive event identification at the EIC.

### Result

The factorization clarifies how the hard-scattering gap spectrum can be computed and distinguished from diffractive production, quantifying the background that must be controlled in EIC measurements.

### Conclusion

A rigorous treatment of rapidity-gap events is needed to disentangle genuine diffraction from ordinary hard scattering in EIC data, motivating refined experimental analyses.

## 360. Why Equal-time Abundance Correlations Alone Do Not Identify Ecological Interactions

- **Tags**: `abundance fluctuations`  `species correlations`  `competition`  `environmental stochasticity`  `population dynamics inference`

### TL;DR

Equal-time abundance correlations in ecological communities cannot by themselves distinguish facilitative environmental responses from competitive resource overlap, so interaction inference from snapshot correlation data is ill-posed.

### Motivation

Empiricists often read positive or negative abundance correlations among species as signatures of, respectively, shared environmental response or competition; the authors ask whether such equal-time correlations actually carry that information.

### Method

Theoretical analysis of stochastic population-dynamics models in which species similarity generates both correlated environmental noise (positive covariance) and competitive coupling (negative covariance), comparing the resulting equal-time abundance correlation structure across scenarios.

### Result

The two mechanisms can produce indistinguishable equal-time correlation patterns, so identical snapshot statistics are compatible with opposite underlying interaction structures unless additional dynamical or temporal information is supplied.

### Conclusion

Identifying ecological interactions requires time-resolved or perturbation data rather than static abundance correlations alone, cautioning against naive correlation-based inference of species interactions.

*Keywords: abundance fluctuations, species correlations, competition, environmental stochasticity, population dynamics inference*

---

Two carbon-deuterium shells were imploded by 14 kJ nanosecond laser beams within dual compressing cones, then heated by a 450 J picosecond laser beam, with diagnostics measuring heating efficiency and shot-to-shot reproducibility.

## 396. Mapping The Limits Of Equilibrium In Sheared Granular Liquid Crystals

- **Score**: 0.4 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Res.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Research
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-11-10
- **Authors**: Jacopo Bilotto, Martin Trulsson, And Jean-François Molinari
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/cjdt-6d9p](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/cjdt-6d9p)
- **Tags**: `granular flows`  `Jeffery orbits`  `liquid crystals`  `shear-induced ordering`  `quasiequilibrium`

### TL;DR

Sheared low-friction granular rods in dense granular flows fail to follow Jeffery orbits and instead settle into a quasiequilibrium state described quantitatively by classical liquid-crystal theory.

### Motivation

Elongated particles suspended in viscous fluids trace Jeffery orbits, but whether this paradigm holds in dense, dissipative granular flows was unresolved, motivating a systematic study of rod dynamics under shear.

### Method

The authors perform particle-scale numerical simulations (discrete element-type modelling) of sheared granular rods with low inter-particle friction, characterizing orientational statistics and comparing them with predictions from classical liquid-crystal theory and Jeffery-orbit kinematics.

### Result

The rods do not follow Jeffery orbits; instead their orientational distribution reaches a quasiequilibrium state that is quantitatively captured by classical liquid-crystal descriptions.

### Conclusion

The work maps the limits of equilibrium-like descriptions in sheared granular media and shows that a liquid-crystal analogy applies to dense granular rod flows, with implications for modelling of granular and soft matter under shear.

*Keywords: granular flows, Jeffery orbits, liquid crystals, shear-induced ordering, quasiequilibrium*

Carbon isotope analyses of undegassed Hawaiian melt inclusions are used to infer that Earth's core contains isotopically heavy carbon.

Most of Earth's carbon is thought to reside in the core, but its composition is inaccessible to direct observation, so indirect geochemical tracers are needed.

### Method

High-precision carbon isotope measurements on undegassed melt inclusions from Hawaiian lavas sampling different mantle source regions, compared with core-formation partitioning experiments and models.

### Result

The melt inclusions carry isotopically heavy carbon signatures consistent with core-mantle partitioning that leaves light carbon in the silicate mantle and heavy carbon in the metal core.

- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2618898123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2618898123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `Greenland ice cores`  `dust provenance`  `Dansgaard–Oeschger events`  `paleoclimate`  `atmospheric dust transport`

### TL;DR

The paper analyzes millennial-scale variability in the provenance of dust in Greenland ice cores during Dansgaard–Oeschger events of the last glacial period.

### Motivation

Greenland ice-core records show dust concentrations up to 15 times higher during cold phases of abrupt glacial climate shifts, and earlier work attributed this dust primarily to East Asian deserts, leaving the source dynamics during these events unresolved.

### Method

Geochemical provenance fingerprinting (e.g., radiogenic isotope and trace-element signatures) of dust from Greenland ice cores, compared against potential source-region signatures, across several Dansgaard–Oeschger events.

### Result

Dust provenance varies on millennial timescales across the stadial–interstadial cycles, indicating shifting dust source contributions rather than a single fixed East Asian source.

### Conclusion

Changes in dust source and transport, rather than concentration alone, characterize abrupt glacial climate transitions, with implications for interpreting atmospheric circulation and dust forcing in ice-core records.

*Keywords: Greenland ice cores, dust provenance, Dansgaard–Oeschger events, paleoclimate, atmospheric dust transport*

- **Tags**: `dio`  `extracellular vesicles`  `efferocytosis`  `idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis`  `don't eat me signal`

### TL;DR

This study shows that extracellular vesicles carrying a 'don't eat me' signal impair efferocytosis and thereby limit resolution of fibrosis in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis.

### Motivation

Idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis is a progressive, fatal disease with no therapies that restore proper tissue repair, and impaired clearance of dying cells (efferocytosis) is implicated in its pathology.

### Method

The authors characterize extracellular vesicles presenting a 'don't eat me' signal and assess their effect on the efferocytic capacity of macrophages, likely using patient samples and murine fibrosis models.

### Result

Vesicles bearing the 'don't eat me' signal suppress efferocytosis, correlating with defective clearance of apoptotic cells and persistent fibrotic remodeling in lung tissue.

### Conclusion

Blocking this vesicle-mediated inhibitory signal may restore efferocytic capacity and offer a therapeutic avenue for resolving lung fibrosis.

*Keywords: extracellular vesicles, efferocytosis, idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis, don't eat me signal, macrophage clearance*

- **Tags**: `epr`  `intestinal stem cells`  `NF-Y transcription factor`  `tissue homeostasis`  `regeneration`

### TL;DR

This study identifies the transcription factor NF-Y as a repressor that keeps intestinal stem cells from hyperproliferating, thereby maintaining tissue homeostasis during regeneration.

### Motivation

Stem cells must balance proliferation against quiescence to preserve tissue homeostasis, but the transcriptional repressors enforcing this balance in the intestinal epithelium are incompletely understood.

### Method

The authors combine mouse genetics and intestinal regeneration models with transcriptional profiling to show that NF-Y directly binds and represses target genes driving stem cell proliferation.

### Result

Loss of NF-Y function releases stem cells from quiescence and drives hyperproliferation, whereas its activity restricts stem cell expansion during regenerative episodes.

### Conclusion

NF-Y acts as a homeostatic brake on intestinal stem cell activity, with implications for understanding epithelial regeneration and proliferation control.

*Keywords: intestinal stem cells, NF-Y transcription factor, tissue homeostasis, regeneration, transcriptional repression*

## 410. Why Ice Is So Slippery

- **Score**: 0.3 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-11-18
- **Authors**: Sigbjørn Løland Bore, B. N. J. Persson, And Henrik Andersen Sveinsson
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/9wv5-ypdm](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/9wv5-ypdm)
- **Tags**: `ice friction`  `premelting film`  `frictional heating`  `nanoscale simulation`  `multiscale modeling`

### TL;DR

Nanoscale simulations of ice-glass friction, upscaled via a frictional-heating model, explain why ice is slippery through thickening of the premelting film.

### Motivation

Nanoscale simulations alone overestimate friction and fail to reproduce its velocity dependence, so a multiscale description of ice friction is needed.

### Method

First-principles nanoscale simulations of ice-glass contacts are combined with a model of frictional heating to upscale the results to macroscopic sliding.

### Result

Frictional heating warms the contact toward the melting point, thickening the premelting film roughly tenfold and reducing friction in a velocity-dependent way.

### Conclusion

The slipperiness of ice arises from a feedback between frictional heating and premelting-film growth, reconciling nanoscale simulation with macroscopic observations.

*Keywords: ice friction, premelting film, frictional heating, nanoscale simulation, multiscale modeling*

The uncertain distribution of stars and compact objects near the Galactic Center introduces timing noise that can limit tests of gravity and black-hole physics using pulsar timing.

### Method

The authors model stochastic mass perturbations from granular structures and quantify their impact on the observed pulse timing residuals for a pulsar-SMBH system.

### Result

### Conclusion

Accurate modeling of Galactic Center mass perturbations is necessary for robust pulsar-based tests of gravity and supermassive black hole properties.

- **Tags**: `shape-memory ceramics`  `transformation-induced plasticity`  `metal matrix composites`  `constraint relief`  `strength-ductility synergy`

### TL;DR

Embedding stimulus-responsive shape-memory ceramics in metals activates transformation-induced plasticity via a constraint-relief mechanism, yielding simultaneous strengthening and retained ductility.

### Motivation

Secondary-phase fillers typically strengthen metals but sacrifice plasticity and functionality, motivating a filler design that instead enhances deformability.

### Method

Shape-memory ceramic particles are incorporated into a metal matrix and the composite's mechanical response during processing and loading is characterized, revealing a constraint-relief mechanism driven by the ceramic's phase transformation.

### Result

The composite exhibits a transformation-induced plasticity response that relieves local microstructural constraints, improving strength–ductility synergy relative to conventional fillers.

### Conclusion

Stimulus-responsive shape-memory ceramics offer a design route for metal matrix composites that avoid the usual strength–plasticity trade-off.

*Keywords: shape-memory ceramics, transformation-induced plasticity, metal matrix composites, constraint relief, strength-ductility synergy*

### TL;DR

The paper reports chemical evidence of heme signatures preserved in mineralized red blood cells inside vascular canals of a 115-million-year-old dinosaur bone.

### Motivation

The work challenges the long-held assumption that original biomolecules inevitably degrade over geological timescales, aiming to show that remnants of blood chemistry can survive in deep-time fossils.

### Method

Organic and isotope geochemical analyses, including high-resolution mass spectrometry and microscopy, were applied to mineralized structures within vascular canals of a Cretaceous dinosaur bone.

### Result

Heme-derived molecular signatures were detected in the mineralized red blood cell-like structures, supporting the survival of original biological molecules over more than 100 million years.

### Conclusion

The findings imply exceptional preservation of biomolecular remnants in Mesozoic fossils and have implications for taphonomy and the interpretation of fossil soft tissues.

- **Tags**: `ancient DNA`  `paleogenomics`  `colonial demography`  `admixture`  `Southern Cone archaeology`

### TL;DR

Genome-wide ancient DNA from Mendoza, Argentina is used to reconstruct demographic dynamics during Spanish colonial urban formation in the Southern Cone.

### Motivation

European colonization reshaped American demographics through settlement, forced migration, and social stratification, yet the colonial Southern Cone remains poorly characterized genomically.

### Method

Ancient DNA was extracted from archaeological human remains in the Mendoza region and analyzed genome-wide, alongside historical and archaeological contextual data.

### Result

The genomes reveal patterns of admixture, population continuity and displacement tied to colonial urban development and labor systems in the region.

### Conclusion

Paleogenomic data illuminate how colonial urbanism restructured Indigenous and admixed populations in the southern Andes.

- **Tags**: `cell crowding`  `myoblasts`  `tissue mechanics`  `cell topology`  `active matter`

### TL;DR

The study shows that elongated muscle precursor cells (myoblasts) respond to crowding through distinct active and passive pathways, producing topological and mechanical differences from epithelial crowding models.

### Motivation

Most physical models of cell crowding are built on polygonal epithelial sheets, leaving unclear how elongated, non-epithelial cells such as muscle precursors behave under dense packing.

### Method

The authors combine experimental characterization of densely packed myoblasts with analysis of cell shape, topology, and mechanical stress, separating active (motility/contractility-driven) from passive (geometry/packing-driven) crowding mechanisms.

### Result

Myoblasts exhibit crowding behavior that diverges from epithelial predictions, with active and passive pathways driving distinct topological signatures and mechanical stress distributions.

### Conclusion

Crowding models developed for epithelia do not transfer directly to elongated cell types; distinguishing active versus passive crowding is necessary to predict tissue mechanics in muscle development and repair.

*Keywords: cell crowding, myoblasts, tissue mechanics, cell topology, active matter*

The authors use genetic and biochemical approaches in mouse models to identify DUSP2 as a phosphatase acting on RPA and to characterize how its loss alters TVM cell activation and antiviral responses in vivo.

- **Tags**: `molybdenum isotopes`  `Neoarchean seawater`  `Great Oxidation Event`  `iron oxidation`  `paleoredox geochemistry`

### TL;DR

A geochemical study using molybdenum isotopes/concentrations in Neoarchean marine sediments to infer extensive iron oxidation and early seawater oxygenation prior to the Great Oxidation Event.

### Motivation

Understanding when and how seawater became oxygenated before the Great Oxidation Event is a key question in reconstructing Earth's environmental evolution, and the spatial extent and persistence of early oxygenated conditions remain poorly constrained.

### Method

The authors analyze molybdenum geochemistry (a 'heavy Mo reservoir') in Neoarchean sedimentary rocks to trace redox conditions and iron oxidation in contemporaneous seawater.

### Result

The heavy Mo signature indicates a substantial oxidized Mo reservoir in Neoarchean seawater, implying more extensive iron oxidation and oxygenated marine conditions than previously recognized.

### Conclusion

Early oxygenation was spatially widespread and persistent, reshaping timelines for the lead-up to the Great Oxidation Event.

*Keywords: molybdenum isotopes, Neoarchean seawater, Great Oxidation Event, iron oxidation, paleoredox geochemistry*

### TL;DR

Cryo-EM structures reveal how the N-terminal region of a mosquito innexin governs heptameric hemichannel assembly and maturation.

### Motivation

Gap junction channels mediate direct intercellular communication, but the assembly mechanisms that produce defined oligomeric architectures remain poorly understood.

### Method

The authors determined cryo-EM structures of a mosquito innexin hemichannel and analyzed the role of the N-terminal segment in oligomerization and maturation.

### Result

The structures show that N-terminal integrity is required for correct heptamer assembly and maturation of the innexin hemichannel.

### Conclusion

These findings clarify the structural basis of innexin gap junction channel assembly and identify the N-terminus as a key determinant of proper oligomerization.

*Keywords: innexin, gap junction, hemichannel, cryo-EM, heptamer assembly*

### TL;DR

This study characterizes the glucocorticoid receptor's role in germinal center B cell homeostasis and plasmacytic differentiation, with relevance to B cell lymphoma biology.

### Motivation

The germinal center is the cellular origin of most B cell lymphomas, and the glucocorticoid receptor is a major pharmacologic target in lymphoid malignancies, yet its physiologic function in GC B cells was unclear.

### Method

The authors combine genetic mouse models and pharmacologic perturbation with transcriptomic and phenotypic analysis of germinal center B cells and plasma cell differentiation.

### Result

GR is shown to regulate GC homeostasis and plasmacytic differentiation, with these functions partly mediated by a functional transcriptional program.

### Conclusion

The findings define a physiologic and pharmacologic role for GR in humoral immunity and suggest implications for glucocorticoid therapy in B cell lymphomas.

Alcohol use disorder frequently co-occurs with anxiety and depression, but the molecular and circuit-level mechanisms coordinating these comorbid phenotypes are poorly understood.

- **Tags**: `histone methyltransferases`  `phylogenetic profiling`  `cytoskeleton`  `evolutionary coupling`  `chromatin regulation`

### TL;DR

Phylogenetic profiling reveals that histone methyltransferases have an evolutionary history linked to cytoskeletal components, suggesting broader roles in coordinating genome organization and cellular architecture beyond chromatin regulation.

### Motivation

Histone methyltransferases are conventionally regarded as chromatin regulators, but the authors hypothesize their evolutionary relationships may reveal additional, unappreciated functions in organizing cellular structure.

### Method

The authors use large-scale phylogenetic profiling—comparing co-occurrence and co-evolution patterns of methyltransferase genes with other genes across many genomes—to identify functional associations.

### Result

Methyltransferase genes show correlated evolutionary profiles with cytoskeleton-related genes, indicating coupled evolutionary pressures and a possible functional link between chromatin-modifying enzymes and cellular architecture.

### Conclusion

The work expands the functional scope of histone methyltransferases to include coordination of genome organization and cytoskeletal architecture, though the claims rest on evolutionary correlation rather than direct mechanistic demonstration.

*Keywords: histone methyltransferases, phylogenetic profiling, cytoskeleton, evolutionary coupling, chromatin regulation*

### TL;DR

A wearable microneedle-based sensor continuously monitors uric acid in interstitial fluid of mice to predict gout flare-associated pain.

### Motivation

Gout flares are painful and unpredictable events that are poorly captured by isolated serum uric acid measurements, motivating continuous, time-resolved biochemical monitoring.

### Method

The authors develop a wearable interstitial fluid sensing platform and apply it to an induced mouse model of gout, tracking uric acid dynamics over time.

### Result

Time-resolved interstitial fluid uric acid profiles resolve dynamic biochemical changes that correlate with the onset of flare-associated pain.

### Conclusion

Continuous uric acid monitoring in interstitial fluid may enable early prediction of gout flares, though validation remains preclinical.

### TL;DR

The plant protein Phytochrome Interacting Factor 7 (PIF7) modulates phytochrome A activity to regulate how plants shift from shade avoidance to canopy adaptation under persistent shade.

### Motivation

Plants must adapt to vegetation shade for survival, and while transient shade triggers shade avoidance, prolonged canopy shade requires a different adaptive strategy whose molecular regulation is poorly understood.

### Method

Molecular genetics and physiological analysis of phytochrome A and PIF7 in Arabidopsis-type model systems under simulated canopy shade conditions, likely including mutant and expression assays.

### Result

PIF7 is shown to moderate phytochrome A activity specifically in persistent canopy shade, tuning the shade-response transition.

### Conclusion

The work identifies a regulatory node controlling the switch between shade avoidance and canopy adaptation, with implications for understanding light-signaling plasticity in plants.

*Keywords: phytochrome A, PIF7, shade avoidance, canopy shade, plant light signaling*
